b3b355d88 · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 18, 17:19

State the invariants of states at every depth, a return to code outside every call included

no-behaviour-change

Why

The invariants of states (Ok) carried the depth of an active call: a member's checked steps kept them only while at least one call of C stayed active, and the one statement of them outside every call was the first entry's, where no protected storage exists (Ok.of_lent). An execution that returns from C to its client and enters C again had no statement of the second entry's conditions, so each later call's bound (exec_bound) rested on a premise nothing established.

addedTheorem the invariants at any depth

Inv(s) = a uniform history, (M), (P2), valid frames
Ok(d, s) ⟺ 0 < d ≤ |A(s)| ∧ Inv(s)(Ok.inv, Inv.ok)

addedTheorem a step keeps them at any depth

PkgStep(d, s, s')Inv(s)⟹Inv(s')(pkgstepinv; d = 0 the last exit)
OutsideStep(Θ, s, s')Inv(s)⟹Inv(s')(outsideinv)

addedTheorem the invariants along an execution

an execution of XStep from a with |A(a)| = depth(a) and Inv(a)
⟹ |A(e)| = depth(e), Inv(e), and Ok(depth(e), e) when depth(e) > 0
(XStep.inv, Ex.inv)

modifiedTheorem the start of an execution

modifiedDocs the read-only handles draft, What the Lean states; What acceptance reads of the rule, 5

the invariants at every depth stated with their theorems; item 5 proved of the paper's executions, reaching the pass with item 2

Soundness

proved in the paper model; no acceptance path reads it.

no-behaviour-change

568139728 · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 18, 18:19

Remove from B what the component no longer holds at each outside step outside every call

no-behaviour-change

Why

Appendix A of the read-only frames note removes from B each location the component no longer holds at a step taken while no call of it is active; the ghost of an execution (Ghost.next) kept B unchanged at every outside step. Holding can decrease there: outside code may overwrite the last reference to a location in storage the component allocated. With B kept, the region would protect such a location at a later entry, and the client obligation Row would bind clients on storage the note does not protect.

modifiedDefinition the ghost after a step of outside code

addedTheorem the trim is the history's drop

A = [] → HStep (alloc g) (outsideNext g)(outsideNextdrop)

addedTheorem the trim keeps the invariants of states

A = [] → Inv st → Inv (st with B := B')(Inv.trim; no invariant reads B)

modifiedTheorem every step keeps the invariants, XStep.inv

the outside case outside every call is the outside step then the trim

modifiedTheorem the decomposition, decompose

modifiedDocs the note, Appendix A, History; the read-only handles draft, actual executions and the invariants at every depth

the removal from B and the Lean that states it

Soundness

paper model only; the region outside every call shrinks to what the component holds, so the client obligation binds no more than the note states.

Locate each step at the node of the code performing it

no-behaviour-change

Why

The checks of a component read each step at its node, and a frame carries no label: the configurations the paper's chk reads do not identify the node a step runs. A located execution threads the labels as a ghost whose erasure is the calculus's step.

addedDefinition the runs of a path and their change at a step

runHead: top | goto code comp label | frame code comp k x
HeadStep p p': tag kept; top → top; frame → frame (same code, k, x);
frame → goto (same code); goto l → frame at a node: K[l] = (k, x), E[l] exists
PathRuns P P': innermost entry changed by HeadStep, or popped and the
next changed; at most one entry pushed, a goto

addedTheorem every step changes the runs so

Step h t h' t' → wf t → HeapFree h → ∀ c, PathRuns (path c t) (path c t')
(stepruns, steprunswf)

modifiedTheorem every step keeps well-formedness, step_wf

addedDefinition located configurations and steps

LabelsOk P ls: one label per entry, a goto's own, a frame's a node
of its code at its continuation and handler
LStep c a b = AStep a.x b.x ∧ Relabel (path c a.x.t) (path c b.x.t) a.ls b.ls
occ: code, tag, label and entry term of the innermost run
allocAt: the i-th location a step allocates

∃ ls', LStep c a ⟨y, ls'⟩ ∧ LabelsOk (path c y.t) ls' (steplift)

d9f53e0cc · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 18, 19:32

Check a proposed flow abstraction as a post-fixed point of constraints

no-behaviour-change

Why

The checks of a component read a finite abstraction of its executions (sites, keys, call targets at each node). An untrusted proposal gives it; soundness needs only that a checked proposal over-approximates every state of an execution, not that it is least.

addedDefinition abstract keys

KeySet = some (finite concrete keys, none among them) | any (every key)
Mem, le (checked inclusion), join

addedTheorem keys

Mem k any; le a b → Mem k a → Mem k b; Mem k a → Mem k (join a b);
Mem k b → Mem k (join a b)

addedDefinition constraints and a checked proposal

Constraint N A = { src dst : N, transfer : A → A }
checkPost le cs sol = ∀ c ∈ cs, le (transfer (sol src)) (sol dst)
Realizes node γ sol s = ∃ n, node s n ∧ γ (sol n) s
Covered R node γ cs: each step from a state at n meeting a is covered
by a constraint from n whose transfer it meets

addedTheorem a checked proposal over-approximates executions

checkPost le cs sol → Covered R node γ cs → le sound for γ →
Ex R q n s e → Realizes s → Realizes e(realizesalong)

modifiedDocs the read-only handles draft, The flow a component's checks read

the proposal, its check, and what remains: the constraints of the lowered graphs and their coverage

Soundness

paper model only; no plan reads it.

Read the checks of C's code from the sites each value may reach

no-behaviour-change

Why

At the actual crossings, ChkSound asks for the marks at each crossing and closed local storage. Both follow from where values can reach: a value whose first locations are of released sites or outside storage, in a heap whose storage references only what its site may, reaches no local, internal or exposed location. The flow of C's code then has one obligation per step: the heap after it meets a heap of sites, and the roots of outside code meet a closed set of lendable sites.

Read SitesSound from the flow at every reached state

no-behaviour-change

Why

SitesSound asks, at each step of C's code from a reached state, for the heap after it meeting the heap of sites and every root of outside code meeting O. The heap follows from fits_step, but the roots did not: a value at a position of outside code could fit any set. The typing now marks the positions of outside code and keeps every set there within O, so a term that fits gives roots in O. Carried along the frame's executions, every reached state fits, and SitesSound follows where the checks give fresh locations the sites the flow reads and every entry fits.

a0214e95f · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 18, 22:13

State the closures an entry may hold by the shapes C's code makes them at

no-behaviour-change

Why

The fit of an entry asks every closure of C's code the entry holds to meet its proposal's entry signatures (CapFn). A closure's captures hold closures of a known signature only where C's code made it so: the module's closure of a function captures the module's closures of the functions it calls; lowered code's closures capture variables. The well-formed entries now state this per closure, by a finite set of shapes, and the checker's condition on the proposal (ShapesOk) gives CapFn for every closure of a shape. Pretext's walkers pass their cursor to the module's helpers at the request's sibling slots, which the calls the claim is about hold as the module's closures; the helpers return primitives, so the return rule needs no change.

9ed8ceb31 · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 18, 22:20

State the checks of a code that give CodeOk, read from its nodes and edges

no-behaviour-change

Why

CodeOk quantifies over every environment meeting a node's slots, so no checker can decide it as stated. The checks state it on the code: each node expression fits openly, its variables past those bound inside it read as the node's slots, a call of a slot naming a signature a call of a closure of C's code; instantiation at an environment meeting the slots keeps it fitting. Each edge carries the node's slots into its target's, slot by slot through its mapping. ContOk quantified over every monotone value judgment; at a branch binding a test's boolean it then asked every judgment to accept true, so no proposal met it (a false obligation: the judgment "not true" is monotone and meets every environment without true). Every step reads it at VJ alone, so it is stated there.

modifiedDefinition ReadOnlyFits.lean

ContOk, HandlerOk: at VJ at every ghost and heap length, in place of
every monotone value judgment

modifiedTheorem ReadOnlyFitsStep.lean

fitsstep: the edges' conditions at VJ

τ i and signature φ i; invokeC at a slot naming a signature
mapIdx, pushSites, pushFn, Carries, CarriesFn: the slot an edge's
target slot reads; slot-wise inclusion of sites and signatures
ContChk, HandlerChk: the checks of a continuation and a handler
CodeChk: the checks of a code

slots and signatures gives Fits

f4684c475 · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 18, 22:39

Decide the checks of a code and the facts of a flow over a finite proposal

no-behaviour-change

Why

CodeChk and Flow.Facts quantify over every slot of every node and every site, so a checker needs a finite form of the proposal and a procedure proved to give them. A finite proposal lists each node's slots with one set past them and the heap of sites as entries; past a width the slots of an edge's ends and its mapping are constant, so finitely many slots decide the edge. A term's least set is computed bottom-up; where it is computed, the term fits it and every larger set.

f72f749fb · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 19, 08:04

Require the frame's check only at nodes that may call outside code, as the callee's set says

no-behaviour-change

Why

The owner's finding 1 (proved in Lean by Astra): an empty site set does not exclude a call of outside code, since a fieldless, capture-free closure of outside code meets the empty set and the call still enters its code. Frame acceptance must hold at every context that can perform the call, and the frame witness must name the node the frame runs. A closure of outside code now meets a set only where the set holds outside storage, its token; a frame records whether its node may call outside code, and a call whose callee's set holds outside storage is allowed only there. At a reached state whose next step calls outside code from a frame of C's code, the frame fits at a node of its continuation and handler that may call outside code.

32afa545d · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 19, 11:26

Design code values that reach no local site as a fact of the value judgment

no-behaviour-change

Why

The handle fixture (bounds-pattern-unreachable-handle-refused.ts) passes the frame's check of both length facts and fails the bindings check at the marks the flow gives: every write is charged to the bindings of the written site, so a local record whose fields hold local arrays has local sites among its bindings, though no bound function or closure of outside code is stored there. The bindings bound a property of the values written, not of the storage.

addedDefinition docs/drafts/readonly-code-values.md

the value judgment gains one conjunct: every value held in the code
(a bound function's parts, a closure of outside code's node values
and captures) of every value within v reaches only allocated,
non-local locations first
the heap of sites keeps its contents alone; Stores s S is S within
the contents of s; a heap meets it when each value meets its site's
contents and its code values reach no local location
makeBound and makeGraphOut: the parts meet a set within the result's
set holding no local site; NoLocal O a flow fact
the checks run at the marks the flow gives

Review

Astra (gpt-6.2-astra, read-only) found it an instance of the heap of sites and (M)'s clause on bindings, general over codeVals and Leaves; three repairs, applied: the conjunct bounds each leaf by the heap's length as well (a counter-model to the origin half alone: a closure of C's code whose node value is a bound function holding .arr [some 1]; checked in Lean: Within, codeVals and Leaves as stated, the closure having no leaf of its own); the parts keep meeting the result's set; the check runs on the remarked flow. Its fourth point, that the refusal of a never-stored binding of a local value is more than (M) asks, is kept as a stated limit: a check at writes needs evidence a set of sites does not carry.

    // A precondition naming an optional or a defaulted parameter: the clause
// reads the argument as the call passes it, `undefined` where the call
// passes none, so it states the cursor's bounds past `c === undefined`. The
// body is checked assuming the clause at its entry, before the default
// replaces an undefined argument, and each call passing the argument
// establishes it (ARITHMETIC.md, "Preconditions").
export type Cursor = { i: number };

/** @requires c === undefined || (safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i && c.i < xs.length) */
export function atOptional(xs: number[], c?: Cursor): number {
  if (c === undefined) return 0;
  return xs[c.i]!;
}

/** @requires c === undefined || (safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i) */
export function atDefault(xs: number[], c: Cursor = { i: 0 }): number {
  return c.i < xs.length ? xs[c.i]! : 0;
}

export function sum(xs: number[]): number {
  let s = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < xs.length; i++) {
    const c: Cursor = { i };
    s = s + atOptional(xs, c) + atDefault(xs, c);
  }
  return s;
}
  • invalid-precondition-optional-parameter negative — → unverified new
    // A call outside an optional parameter's precondition: `atOptional(xs, c)`
// with `c.i` at the array's length reads past the end, so `last([1, 2])`
// returns `undefined` as a `number` (run under Node).
export type Cursor = { i: number };

/** @requires c === undefined || (safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i && c.i < xs.length) */
export function atOptional(xs: number[], c?: Cursor): number {
  if (c === undefined) return 0;
  return xs[c.i]!;
}

export function last(xs: number[]): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: xs.length };
  return atOptional(xs, c);
}
  • bounds-pattern-optional-requires positive gap → positive unsupported → verified the gate admits the optional parameter
    // A precondition on a function with an optional parameter: `at` reads
// `xs[i]` below the length its `@requires` states, and its last parameter is
// optional. The clause names the required parameters as the call passes
// them, and the optional one admits `undefined`.
// Pretext: line-break.ts walkPreparedLinesRaw (an optional visitor and a
// defaulted stats) and walkPreparedComplexLines, whose cursor and handle
// relations are their callers' to establish.
/** @requires safeInteger(i) && 0 <= i && i < xs.length */
export function at(xs: number[], i: number, scale?: number): number {
  return xs[i]! * (scale ?? 1);
}
  • bounds-default-precondition-gap gap unsupported → unsupported the unguarded path through the defaulted cursor is refused
    // Pretext layoutNextRichInlineLineRange's start cursor: supplied and default values share a bound.
/** @requires safeInteger(cursor.index) && 0 <= cursor.index */
export function cursorIndex(cursor: { index: number } = { index: 0 }): number {
  return cursor.index;
}
export function entry(): number { return cursorIndex(); }

Gap

A call passing no argument for a position the clause names is refused: the check reads facts at the positions the call passes. Establishing a clause the default meets needs the clause checked of the default after parameter initialization (bounds-default-precondition-gap). A closure's precondition still needs plain required parameters.

Soundness

The clause is elaborated over the parameter's optional type, so a path through it is read only past a guard against undefined. The body is checked from its entry, where the slot holds the actual; the default replaces it afterwards by an ordinary binding. Each call establishes the facts at its passed positions, and a call passing fewer is refused.

Counterexample

const c: Cursor = { i: xs.length }; return atOptional(xs, c)Node: last([1, 2]) prints undefined, returned at number.

a30012a57 · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 19, 14:56

Design the framed plan and its theorem, closing the five gaps to acceptance

no-behaviour-change

Why

The read-only frame rule is proved at the paper's executions, but no unit is accepted through it: FunctionPlan has the graph form alone. P22's walker (bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused) stays a gap. A review of an acceptance design found five gaps between the proved results and a verdict: the frame is checked at the state before the callee is invoked while the rule's consequence reads the state after it; a callback may shorten an array and throw, and the segment is read at one effect; a re-entered member's fresh arguments have no protection witness; a certificate's guarantee ranges over every checked step, which no code makes; the guarantee route's ownership lemma takes two unproved premises. Under Node a shortening, throwing visitor makes the walker's handler read xs[1] of an emptied array.

addedDocs the framed acceptance design draft

A framed call is a call whose run may reach outside code, a function of the component that calls a client (a wrapper) among them. Across it, from the call's own state to the frame's resumption, every location of R_C changes within Psi_C join Theta_C join W_c, W_c the charges of the component's writes the callee runs at the caller's depth, and every other location within the callee's effect: Phi at a normal return, Phi^e (top where no exceptional bound is stated) at a throw. The check runs at every node and context sharing the call's continuation and handler. Certificates are read at the call's own trace. No fact of a re-entered call is read by the unit's claim, so the witnesses are the entry's. The guarantee route gets its two premises from path supports and the classification of every write the support reads. The graph theorem is stated relative to the framed returns, and the six framed clauses are built from it by induction on the budget.

modifiedDocs the framed plan of the read-only handles draft

Counterexample

walk(xs, i => { if (i === 1) { xs.length = 0; throw new Error() } })with walk's handler reading xs[c.i]Node: { s: NaN, i: 2 }

Prove that an assertion holding on graph paths holds at every state the term reaches

no-behaviour-change

Why

The framed claim's clauses read a stated assertion at every state an execution reaches (AssertedAt as the J of SafeUnder), while the pass's graph theorems state assertions along graph paths (ReachesIn). The framed acceptance draft's framed_requestSafe and the codes' clause of its obligations need the direction from graph paths to the term's executions, the converse of asserted_of_reach.

addedTheorem a term's path is a graph path

Steps j h ts h' ts'Sim E K ts s
∃ s'. ReachesIn (program E K) j h s h' s' ∧ Sim E K ts' s'
(sim_reaches)

addedTheorem assertions at every state reached

∀ (l, k) ∈ A, j < n, ReachesIn P j h (at start ρ₀) h' (at l ρ') ⟹ k holds
∀ j < n, Steps j h (gotoOf P start L d c) h' t' ⟹ AssertedAt P A ρ h h' t'
with ρ₀ = envOf L d

State the graph machine's safety at framed returns, its safety the instance with no framed node

no-behaviour-change

Why

The framed acceptance draft's RunSoundFramed gives the main graph's clauses as safety at framed returns: a run is followed past the transition leaving a framed node only where that return meets Ret (normal exit) or RetE (throw), from the heap the node was entered at. The graph theorem's composition (graph_step, graph_safe) required every node's exits to reach their targets unconditionally, so a framed call node, whose kept facts hold only where its return meets Ret, could not be composed. Mirroring the composition for framed runs would duplicate it; generalizing it once keeps one proof.

addedDefinition safety of the graph machine at framed returns

SafeGraphRet Ξ P T F Ret RetE n W p h s: SafeGraph, with the run
followed past a transition only where it meets the returns of the

(l, h) (enterPend), a step of its term keeps it, any other transition
clears it (pendG)

addedDefinition obligations under return premises

NodeObligationsR: NodeObligations with each exit's target required
where the exit's heap meets Rn (normal) or Re (throw);
NodesCheckedR P pre F Ret RetE: a framed node's premises are Ret and
RetE from the heap it is entered at; GraphInvR, EvalOkR: the
invariant under a pending evaluation

addedTheorem one transition, at framed returns

NodesCheckedR P pre F Ret RetEAdminClosed preGraphInvR p (n+1) W h st
terminal ∨ ∃ st'. Transition h st h' st' ∧
(RetAtG p h' st st' → GraphInvR (pendG F p h st st') n U h' st')
(graph_stepR)

addedTheorem graph safety at framed returns

NodesCheckedR P pre F Ret RetE ⟹ GraphInvR p ⟹ SafeGraphRet F Ret RetE p
(graphsafeR, graphsafeatR); SafeGraphRet with no framed node and
nothing pending is SafeGraph (SafeGraphRet.erase)

modifiedTheorem graph_step, graph_safe

Effect

  • bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused gap refused → refused its framed plan's binding checks pass, its framed walk is accepted, refused at FramedObligations.reached
    // A fact survives a callback that cannot reach what the fact is about.
//
// This module is a component: the code in it is inside, all other code is
// outside, and outside code enters only through its exports, here `walk`.
// The visitor `visit` is outside code. Its type states its effect with the
// verifier's annotation `Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>`
// (`void` is its return type): of what it can reach, it may change any
// element or field to a value its type allows (`values`), and may lengthen,
// never shorten, any array (`spines`: array lengths).
//
// Across the call `visit(c.i)` the verifier keeps `c.i < xs.length`, checking
// the two locations the fact depends on:
// - `c.i`: `c` is allocated in `walk` and never given to outside code, so no
//   outside code can reach it, and its field is unchanged;
// - the length of `xs`: `xs` came from outside, so the visitor may reach it,
//   and its stated effect lets it only grow.
// With `0 <= c.i` from the loop invariant, the read `xs[c.i]` after the call
// is in bounds.
type Visitor = (w: number) => Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>;
type Cursor = { i: number };

export function walk(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 }; // never given to outside code
  let s = 0;
  // `@invariant`: a loop invariant the verifier checks; `safeInteger(c.i)`:
  // `c.i` is an integer within Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, so `c.i++` is exact.
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c.i); // c.i unchanged; xs may only grow
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s;
}
  • invalid-bounds-pattern-cursor-given negative refused → refused its framed walk refused at node 10
    // The walker of bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused.ts, but the cursor
// is given to the visitor: the visitor may change `c.i` through its own
// alias, as its stated effect allows (`values: "any"`), so `c.i < xs.length`
// is lost at the visit, and the read `xs[c.i]` after it may be past the
// array. Under Node, `walk([1, 2, 3], (c) => { c.i = 5; })` reads `xs[5]`,
// `undefined`, and returns NaN (tests/node-runs.mts).
type Cursor = { i: number };
type Visitor = (c: Cursor) => Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>;

export function walk(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 }; // given to outside code at each visit
  let s = 0;
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c);
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s;
}

Gap

FramedObligations.reached (ret_reached) and the theorems of track B4 stand between P22 and acceptance; then the framed branch accepts and Mode8Accepted's print site reads the framed plan's group.

  • bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused gap refused → refused unchanged line: binding checks pass, framed walk accepted, refused at FramedObligations.reached
    // A fact survives a callback that cannot reach what the fact is about.
//
// This module is a component: the code in it is inside, all other code is
// outside, and outside code enters only through its exports, here `walk`.
// The visitor `visit` is outside code. Its type states its effect with the
// verifier's annotation `Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>`
// (`void` is its return type): of what it can reach, it may change any
// element or field to a value its type allows (`values`), and may lengthen,
// never shorten, any array (`spines`: array lengths).
//
// Across the call `visit(c.i)` the verifier keeps `c.i < xs.length`, checking
// the two locations the fact depends on:
// - `c.i`: `c` is allocated in `walk` and never given to outside code, so no
//   outside code can reach it, and its field is unchanged;
// - the length of `xs`: `xs` came from outside, so the visitor may reach it,
//   and its stated effect lets it only grow.
// With `0 <= c.i` from the loop invariant, the read `xs[c.i]` after the call
// is in bounds.
type Visitor = (w: number) => Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>;
type Cursor = { i: number };

export function walk(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 }; // never given to outside code
  let s = 0;
  // `@invariant`: a loop invariant the verifier checks; `safeInteger(c.i)`:
  // `c.i` is an integer within Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, so `c.i++` is exact.
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c.i); // c.i unchanged; xs may only grow
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s;
}
  • bounds-pattern-framed-protocol-refused gap new its framed walk refused at the visit
    // The walker of bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused beside an exported
// function that writes an element of a caller's array. The component's
// protocol Ψ_C (docs/drafts/framed-acceptance.md, "Charges") changes the
// elements of storage outside code may hold: a framed plan states it on
// the wire, and the pass checks the wire's at least that
// (`protocolAtLeastB`). The walker's framed plan passes its binding checks
// and its framed walk is refused at the visit; its ordinary plan is refused
// at the read after the visit.
type Visitor = (w: number) => Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>;
type Cursor = { i: number };

export function walk(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 };
  let s = 0;
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c.i);
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s;
}

export function clear(xs: number[]): void {
  if (xs.length > 0) xs[0] = 0;
}
  • invalid-guard-constant-across-loops negative refused → refused within its limit (2.7 s; A5's attempt ran past 60 s)
    // A guard's answer held in a constant across forty loops, each loop head a
// cut, then an element read typed `number` that an empty array leaves
// `undefined`: `walk([], undefined)` returns `undefined` under Node. The
// guarded add needs the implication the loop heads carry, which the search's
// later rounds seed; every round fails at the read, which is the one
// reported, over the first round's failure at the add's edge. The function lowers to
// about 1,800 nodes, and each failing round searches all of them.

  const first: number = xs[0];
  return total > 0 ? total : first;
}

  • bounds-pattern-framed-shared-code-refused gap new both walkers' framed plans pass their binding checks, framed walks accepted, refused at FramedObligations.reached
    // P22's walker (bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused) twice, under two
// names, beside a module function both call.
//
// A code of the component is a term: `walk` and `walkAgain` have the same
// code, so they are one code of the component's flow of sites, with one
// pair of allocation sites per allocation and one closure token, and the
// initialization allocates a closure of that code for each of them, each
// capturing `total`'s closure. Each walker keeps `c.i < xs.length` across
// the call `visit(c.i)` as P22's does.
type Visitor = (w: number) => Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>;
type Cursor = { i: number };

export function total(xs: number[]): number {
  let s = 0;
  for (let k = 0; k < xs.length; k++) s += xs[k]!;
  return s;
}

export function walk(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 }; // never given to outside code
  let s = 0;
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c.i); // c.i unchanged; xs may only grow
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s + total(xs);
}

export function walkAgain(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 }; // never given to outside code
  let s = 0;
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c.i); // c.i unchanged; xs may only grow
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s + total(xs);
}
  • bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused gap refused → refused unchanged line
    // A fact survives a callback that cannot reach what the fact is about.
//
// This module is a component: the code in it is inside, all other code is
// outside, and outside code enters only through its exports, here `walk`.
// The visitor `visit` is outside code. Its type states its effect with the
// verifier's annotation `Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>`
// (`void` is its return type): of what it can reach, it may change any
// element or field to a value its type allows (`values`), and may lengthen,
// never shorten, any array (`spines`: array lengths).
//
// Across the call `visit(c.i)` the verifier keeps `c.i < xs.length`, checking
// the two locations the fact depends on:
// - `c.i`: `c` is allocated in `walk` and never given to outside code, so no
//   outside code can reach it, and its field is unchanged;
// - the length of `xs`: `xs` came from outside, so the visitor may reach it,
//   and its stated effect lets it only grow.
// With `0 <= c.i` from the loop invariant, the read `xs[c.i]` after the call
// is in bounds.
type Visitor = (w: number) => Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>;
type Cursor = { i: number };

export function walk(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 }; // never given to outside code
  let s = 0;
  // `@invariant`: a loop invariant the verifier checks; `safeInteger(c.i)`:
  // `c.i` is an integer within Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, so `c.i++` is exact.
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c.i); // c.i unchanged; xs may only grow
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s;
}

dca7a1a31 · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 20, 01:16

Prove the returns from the checks at each framed node, and show an entering step may write

no-behaviour-change

Why

The returns a framed node's exits rely on follow from the transfer at each framed node, whose own premises are the frame's fit, the binding checks and the pass's frame check. The fit of a frame records its node through its continuation and handler alone, so the sets it gives the operands are those of some node sharing them, at some context; a call node's set holding the operand slots' sets at every such node and context bounds the call's values in depth, which is what the own steps read. The members' certificates stay a premise: the planned argument that an entering step changes no location fails for well-formed terms, since a step of outside code evaluating a write just before a pending run of the component enters the call while it writes, and neither the client obligation nor the component's checks read that step.

  • bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused gap refused at FramedObligations.reached → refused at FramedObligations (the frame's invariants)
    // A fact survives a callback that cannot reach what the fact is about.
//
// This module is a component: the code in it is inside, all other code is
// outside, and outside code enters only through its exports, here `walk`.
// The visitor `visit` is outside code. Its type states its effect with the
// verifier's annotation `Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>`
// (`void` is its return type): of what it can reach, it may change any
// element or field to a value its type allows (`values`), and may lengthen,
// never shorten, any array (`spines`: array lengths).
//
// Across the call `visit(c.i)` the verifier keeps `c.i < xs.length`, checking
// the two locations the fact depends on:
// - `c.i`: `c` is allocated in `walk` and never given to outside code, so no
//   outside code can reach it, and its field is unchanged;
// - the length of `xs`: `xs` came from outside, so the visitor may reach it,
//   and its stated effect lets it only grow.
// With `0 <= c.i` from the loop invariant, the read `xs[c.i]` after the call
// is in bounds.
type Visitor = (w: number) => Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>;
type Cursor = { i: number };

export function walk(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 }; // never given to outside code
  let s = 0;
  // `@invariant`: a loop invariant the verifier checks; `safeInteger(c.i)`:
  // `c.i` is an integer within Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, so `c.i++` is exact.
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c.i); // c.i unchanged; xs may only grow
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s;
}
  • bounds-pattern-framed-shared-code-refused gap the same

Read a framed plan's returns along the program, left with two premises its initialization falsifies

gap
pipeline/examples/bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused.ts#walk

Why

ret_reachedW_public reads the frame's invariants (the slots' values, ChkSound, StartInv, EntryFits, NoKeys) along the program from its initial configurations, so FramedObligations.reached uses it and the framed claim's obligations are the two premises left on the initial configurations: each fits the flow (InitialFits) and is an entry (InitialEntry). Both read the initialization's code at the flow: the initial term runs it at a tag of C, so provenance needs it among the codes the flow proposes for and its fit needs a proposal (gotoC). The flow proposes for no initialization code, and could not as the pass builds it: every flow code is lowered code (codesLoweredB), and initB requires each capture of a realm value to be the realm's identity, a located value, which lowered code does not hold. P22's walk names two realm values. So the premises are false at the pass's checks, and the draft states the design that makes them checks: the initialization a lowered code of C the flow proposes for, reading the realm's identities from its slots.

Effect

  • bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused gap refused at FramedObligations (the frame's invariants) → refused at FramedObligations (InitialFits, InitialEntry)
    // A fact survives a callback that cannot reach what the fact is about.
//
// This module is a component: the code in it is inside, all other code is
// outside, and outside code enters only through its exports, here `walk`.
// The visitor `visit` is outside code. Its type states its effect with the
// verifier's annotation `Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>`
// (`void` is its return type): of what it can reach, it may change any
// element or field to a value its type allows (`values`), and may lengthen,
// never shorten, any array (`spines`: array lengths).
//
// Across the call `visit(c.i)` the verifier keeps `c.i < xs.length`, checking
// the two locations the fact depends on:
// - `c.i`: `c` is allocated in `walk` and never given to outside code, so no
//   outside code can reach it, and its field is unchanged;
// - the length of `xs`: `xs` came from outside, so the visitor may reach it,
//   and its stated effect lets it only grow.
// With `0 <= c.i` from the loop invariant, the read `xs[c.i]` after the call
// is in bounds.
type Visitor = (w: number) => Modifies<{ values: "any"; spines: "grow" }, void>;
type Cursor = { i: number };

export function walk(xs: number[], visit: Visitor): number {
  const c: Cursor = { i: 0 }; // never given to outside code
  let s = 0;
  // `@invariant`: a loop invariant the verifier checks; `safeInteger(c.i)`:
  // `c.i` is an integer within Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, so `c.i++` is exact.
  /** @invariant safeInteger(c.i) && 0 <= c.i */
  while (c.i < xs.length) {
    visit(c.i); // c.i unchanged; xs may only grow
    s += xs[c.i]!;
    c.i++;
  }
  return s;
}
  • bounds-pattern-framed-shared-code-refused gap the same

632a936d2 · Cristiano Calcagno · Nov 20, 04:53

Show that no lowered code creates a closure whose code names a signature at a capture

gap
pipeline/examples/bounds-pattern-unreachable-field-refused.ts#walk

Why

The draft's fix reads the initialization as a code of C the flow proposes for, its captures of realm values the variables of its slots, and states that its node expressions are then lowered code. Measured at P22, with the initialization added to componentCodes as a unit: FFlow.check refuses its node 0, the creation of walk's closure, and codesLoweredB refuses the same node. walk's entry names %ElementAt%'s signature at its capture slot 4 (the seed sibSig gives, by which the read xs[c.i]! is a known call), and both Lowered and the open fit of a closure creation (OFits.makeGraphC, decided by graphSites's selfFnB) require that only the closure's own slot names a signature. The premise is false; the semantic rule Fits.makeGraphC reads CapFn at the instantiated captures and is no obstacle. The draft's design now replaces SelfFn by CapFn read on the open term (a capture slot names a signature where the capture is a slot of the node naming it) and states CodesLowered as its one consumer reads it (node expressions holding no located value). With both relaxed executably (measurement only, reverted), P22's component checks pass, the initialization's proposal returns within O, and the plan reaches FramedObligations.

Why

With the initial configurations' premises proved from the component's checks, FramedAccepted is everything a framed member's safety reads, and each of its parts is a check the pass runs or the framed walk's acceptance. The framed plan's run now accepts where every check passes: its walk settled at some candidates with no fallback (the framed claim reads the direct walk), its request's checks at those candidates. passMember_sound's framed case relates the accepted run to FramedAccepted at the request with the settled candidates, which reads no candidate for safety (framedRequestSafe_recandidated). The CLI accepts the unit at that framed plan, a print site of Mode8Accepted, and its verdict line names the component whose client obligation it is accepted under. P22 is accepted under client trust at component 0; its walker given the cursor stays refused (NaN under Node). The record's outcome is gap: an acceptance under client trust is not verified, the outcome the hook's list has no word for.
